# Generate An Experimental Parity-Augmented HYPER Matrix

Construct a package-specific experimental extension that augments a base
HYPER matrix with additional parity-derived rows. This constructor is
not the Hong et al. HYPER procedure and is documented as an experimental
package extension only.

## Usage

``` r
hyper_ec_matrix(
  n,
  k = NULL,
  p = 0.01,
  m_base = NULL,
  q = 2L,
  alpha = 0.2,
  sparse_parity = TRUE,
  parity_weight = 3L
)
```

## Arguments

- n:

  Integer scalar giving the total number of individuals \\N\\. This is
  the column dimension of the base matrix \\\mathbf{M}\_{base}\\ and the
  combined matrix.

- k:

  Optional integer scalar used as a sizing heuristic for the expected
  number of positive individuals. If `NULL`, the function derives \\k =
  \max\\1, \lfloor pN \rfloor\\\\ from `p`.

- p:

  Numeric scalar giving the expected prevalence used only when `k` is
  `NULL`. The default is `0.01`.

- m_base:

  Optional integer scalar giving the number of base HYPER pools \\J_0\\.
  If `NULL`, the function uses its current package heuristic based on
  `n`, `k`, and `q`.

- q:

  Integer scalar giving the number of base HYPER pools containing each
  individual. Allowed values are `1L`, `2L`, and `3L`. The default is
  `2L`.

- alpha:

  Numeric scalar giving the parity-overhead fraction used to set \\K =
  \lceil \alpha J_0 \rceil\\. It must lie in \\(0,1)\\.

- sparse_parity:

  Logical scalar; if `TRUE`, generate a sparse random parity-check
  matrix \\\mathbf{H}\\. Otherwise generate a dense random binary
  matrix.

- parity_weight:

  Integer scalar giving the target row weight of the sparse parity-check
  matrix when `sparse_parity = TRUE`.

## Value

A named list with components:

- matrix:

  Combined sparse binary matrix with the \\J_0\\ base rows followed by
  the \\K\\ parity-derived rows

- matrix_base:

  Base HYPER matrix \\\mathbf{M}\_{base}\\

- matrix_parity:

  Parity-check matrix \\\mathbf{H}\\

- m:

  Total number of rows \\J = J_0 + K\\

- m_base:

  Number of base rows \\J_0\\

- k_parity:

  Number of parity rows \\K\\

- k_design:

  The sizing heuristic \\k\\ actually used

- n:

  Number of individuals \\N\\

- alpha:

  Parity-overhead fraction

- q:

  Base HYPER column weight

- lambda_max:

  Estimated maximum number of rows containing an individual

- error_correction:

  Maximum error weight searched by the current decoder

- efficiency:

  Realized ratio \\J/N\\

- sparse_parity:

  Whether sparse parity construction was used

- parity_weight:

  Requested sparse parity row weight

- design_type:

  "hyper_ec"

The combined matrix also carries a `design` attribute mirroring the main
structural fields.

## Scope

The package function `hyper_ec_matrix()` is an experimental convenience
wrapper around two pieces:

1.  a base HYPER matrix \\\mathbf{M}\_{base} \in \\0,1\\^{J_0 \times
    N}\\ generated by
    [`hyper_matrix`](https://myaseen208.github.io/ppgt/reference/hyper_matrix.md),
    and

2.  a binary parity-check matrix \\\mathbf{H} \in \\0,1\\^{K \times
    J_0}\\.

The returned matrix stacks the base rows and derived parity rows. The
parity rows are constructed in code by \$\$\mathbf{M}\_{parity} =
(\mathbf{H}\mathbf{M}\_{base}) \bmod 2.\$\$

This package-specific mod-2 construction should be treated as
experimental. It is not part of the HYPER design described in Hong et
al. (2022), which is a two-stage procedure with conservative stage-1
decoding followed by individual retesting in stage 2.

## Current Interpretation

The current implementation uses `k` or `p` only as heuristics for sizing
the base design. The returned object exposes:

- a base HYPER assignment matrix,

- a randomly generated parity-check matrix, and

- a derived matrix of parity rows used by
  [`hyper_ec_decode()`](https://myaseen208.github.io/ppgt/reference/hyper_ec_decode.md).

The stored field `error_correction = floor(k_parity / 2)` is the maximum
error weight that the current brute-force search will attempt. It is not
a proven correction guarantee.

## References

Hong, F. S., Shah, N., Briggs, A., et al. (2022). HYPER group testing
for SARS-CoV-2: A flexible, easy-to-implement, and highly efficient
method. *Nature Communications*, 13, Article 3626.

The paper above describes HYPER itself. The parity augmentation
implemented here is package-specific and experimental.

# Experimental parity-augmented HYPER design
design1 <- hyper_ec_matrix(n = 256, k = 2, q = 2, alpha = 0.20)
